## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

DERIVATION.md gives the full finite-sum proof. Let b_t be the quadratic bent sign on even t bits, A_j the origin indicator on a fresh r-bit block, and p=(1-chi_[m])/2 the shared m-bit parity event. For fresh selector z_j, define h_j=b_r(x_j)(-1)^(A_j z_j) and

    f=b_m(y) product_j b_r(x_j) (-1)^[p(y) sum_j A_j z_j].

The signed Fourier rows b_m(1+chi)/2 and b_m(1-chi)/2 have disjoint support, each flat with half total mass. Therefore H(f)=m+L[r+H(h_j)]/2 and I(f)=m/2+L[r/2+(r+1)2^-r/2]. This proves H/I<=9/4 for all even r>=4; the exact r=4 module entropy further gives H<=2I. The family is not an FEI counterexample.

For r=4, q=1/16 and the module erasure is beta=1+(39/64)log2(13)-(45/64)log2(3)-(25/32)log2(5), with 0.326522370791<beta<0.326522370792. Its beta>1/4 sign is the integer comparison 2^48*13^39>3^45*5^50. At EVERY selector-first prefix history, d=q/2, B=beta/2, W=q(m+4)/2, R=q(m-1)/2. The exact spectral sector factors over all other module variables; no incoherent mixture or unlabelled histogram replaces the signed rows. All these selectors are strictly bad at K=4.

With L=m even, I(f)=85m/32. The selector-first prefix raw localized bill is m(m+4)/32, hence ratio (m+4)/85 grows without bound. Retaining M and sigma but omitting R still gives prefix [W-M-sigma-(4-log2(e))d]_+ summed to m[(beta-1/4)/2+(m-1)/32], whose ratio also diverges. This is a genuine obstruction to that precise currency and EVERY-tree quantifier. It is not an invalid triangle-inequality argument: W was computed from the exact signed convolution and physical child product. The true all-credit prefix [B-4d]_+ instead has ratio (16/85)(beta-1/4)<0.014404210973, independent of m.

For every finite fixed threshold K, even r large enough makes beta_r/2^-r>K. The independently reconstructed bent-sector chi-square bound gives beta_r/2^-r>=r-4*2^-r log2(e)>r-1. This extends the shared-phase R-dropped obstruction to every fixed K, while the explicit entropy ratio remains <=9/4. The broad raw quantifier failure is an alternative witness to A021's known result, not a new general no-go claim.

For r=4,L=m>=8, a COMPLETE good order queries all shared core bits first, then all selectors, then each four-bit module tail. At core nodes d is 1/2, mu=[1-(15/16)^m]/2, or 1-mu. The exact inequalities 3*16^8>5*15^8 and 5^5<2^12 show d>1/5 and B<=h2(d)<4d. Core nodes are good. Selector nodes remain bad only in the exposed p=1 branches (total probability 1/2), giving raw bill m/8 and exact ratio 4/85. All remaining module tails are good, as shown analytically and rechecked by 216 partial-assignment splits. Before those tails, actual mean terminal I is 17m/8, actual mean terminal H is m[3+H(b4')/4], and I_removed=m/2+m/32. Each such leaf has H<=2I. Both the stopping boundary and the subsequent complete tree are accounted for; terminal entropy is never set to zero before completion.

There is also an exact adaptive selector-only interface. If selectors are queried only between completed quadratic core pairs, let U_j be the physical number of shared core bits still free when selector j is queried. Then the raw selector bill equals q/2[4L+sum_j E U_j], whereas the all-credit selector bill equals L(beta-4q)/2, regardless of this prefix interleaving. This extends A021's fresh-control exposure idea to shared phase reuse. It does not pay the intervening core nodes: after conditioning on selector outcomes, core-node influences and credits can change. Exact pair-boundary Fourier admission is essential; arbitrary nonlinear shared phases need not give disjoint rows.

The first unsupported universal line remains an influence-paid selection/ownership rule for arbitrary Boolean nodes, including colliding shared-phase rows and terminal phase entropy. No passing finite test or proper-class order establishes that universal implication.

# A036 / B08: normalized collision payment on point-defect tensors

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

All logarithms are base two unless written ln. Let C be the class of all finite disjoint tensor products of f_m(x)=1-2*1_{x=x0}, m>=2, allowing arbitrary physical translations, a global output sign, character multiplication, extra character coordinates and irrelevant coordinates. All functions are genuine Boolean signs on the uniform cube. Define P(S)=fhat(S)^2, d_i=P(i in S), I=sum_i d_i, B=sum_i h_2(d_i), and the reference Q(S)=prod_i d_i^{1_{i in S}}(1-d_i)^{1_{i not in S}}. Q is fixed across sampled spectral sets. On its support define b_S=sign(fhat(S))*sqrt(Q(S)), and set b_S=0 off that support. For this class P and Q have identical support, so no sign completion is needed.

Put K=D_2(P||Q), C_b(U)=sum_S b_S b_{S xor U}, v_U=C_b(U)^2, Z=sum_U v_U, L=sum_U v_U log_2(1/v_U), R=v/Z and T=L/Z=H(R)-log_2 Z. Zero summands are zero. The following three inequalities hold on C:

1. **Named excess payment:** K >= [B-4I]_+.
2. **Normalized collision payment:** K >= T/4-8I.
3. **Separate sufficient recovery:** B <= T/4+8I.

Thus collision payment with any constant kappa on this class would imply H(f)=B-K <= (kappa+8)I; the proved kappa=8 yields 16I. Independently, the proof of (1) already gives the stronger H<=4I. The contribution is the explicit divergence/collision ledger and the recovery estimate, not a new FEI class claim or a novelty claim for the known point-defect entropy bound. Constants are conservative.

These estimates are NOT asserted for arbitrary Boolean functions, arbitrary sparse activation of a seed, physical restrictions, overlapping products, or near-tensor surgery. In particular the full-support low-I universal collision premise remains open.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

Execution protocol: fei-execution-protocol/2, revision 2026-10-11-execution-v2, read in full. Read campaign README/CURRENT_STATUS and inspected the claim, obstruction and packet registries, next-action file and plan, particularly plan normalization paragraph and A036 acceptance contract. BOARD.md was read at stage start; B08 STREAM_NOTES.md was absent at both checks. No earlier local A036 output existed.

Supplement: CAMPAIGN.md, CLAIMS.md, COUNTEREXAMPLES.md, SEED_LEMMAS.md and NATIVE_MAPPING.md were inspected. Most relevant input is SEED_LEMMAS Section 3, the exact point-defect law and fixed-Renyi obstruction. The curated ROUTE_MAP_CURATED.md Sections 1-4 and 9.1 identify the prior normalized convolution/support-loss controls and the still-open KL premise. Its source blob is 216cb662e7b21cfe6d7c2034a6690f3c10b886fa. The referenced unbundled September normalized-convolution and collision-admission reports were NOT retrieved in full, so they are not proof dependencies here. Their scope warnings are retained; all formulas needed below are independently derived.

Read relevant contextual excerpts from threshold_paper_38pp.txt (Theorem A, Proposition 5.5 and Theorem 6.6) and the earlier 29-page threshold version (Theorem A); the current threshold constant differs from the earlier version. Read percolation_spectral_pivotal_entropy_30pp.txt Section 11.2/Corollary 11.3 and the corresponding Section 11.2 in the 31-page version. The latter comparison illustrates the importance of direction and support in relative entropy, and is not a mathematical premise of this proof. Full texts were searched for reference/divergence/composition locators; this report does not claim a full independent audit of these manuscripts. Earlier B05/B09/B19 reports were inspected for prior obstruction/review scope; none supplies a proof premise below. Local hashes of the contextual inputs are recorded in out/source_receipts.json.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Exact reference, support and cross entropy

For any law P, its product-marginal Q satisfies E_P log_2(1/Q)=B and H(P)+D_2(P||Q)=B. For a point-defect factor let p=2^-m, d=2p=2^(1-m). Taking x0=all+,

fhat(empty)=1-d, fhat(S)=-d for S nonempty;
P(empty)=(1-d)^2, P(S)=d^2 for S nonempty;
I_i=d, I=md, Q(S)=d^{|S|}(1-d)^{m-|S|}.

All coefficients are nonzero for m>=2. The reference is normalized: sum Q=1. For a disjoint tensor, both P and Q tensorize and b tensorizes. Character/irrelevant coordinates are deterministic under both laws; they add no entropy/divergence. For A=supp P, Q(A)=1. Thus K_supp=-log_2 Q(A)=0 and K_shape=D_2(P||Q(.|A))=K_full=K. This explicitly accounts for support terms rather than discarding them.

Character multiplication permutes spectral indices and sends d_i to 1-d_i on affected coordinates. B, K and squared autocorrelations are unchanged. Since the unshifted factor has d<=1/2, influence weakly increases under character multiplication. Physical translations multiply b_S by a character in S, which multiplies C_b(U) by a sign and leaves v unchanged. Therefore it suffices to prove the estimates for unshifted all+ point defects.

### 2. Exact collision formula and tensor normalization

Write c_S=sqrt(Q(S)); c_empty=(1-d)^(m/2). Then b=-c+2c_empty*delta_empty. For U nonempty of size k,

C_b(U)=(2*sqrt(d(1-d)))^k -4(1-d)^(m-k/2)*d^(k/2)
       =(2*sqrt(d(1-d)))^k [1-2^(2-k)(1-d)^(m-k)].

Let a=4d(1-d). Consequently

v_0=1,
v_k=a^k [1-2^(2-k)(1-d)^(m-k)]^2, k>=1,
Z=1+sum_{k=1}^m binom(m,k)v_k,
L=sum_{k=1}^m binom(m,k)v_k log_2(1/v_k).

For k=1 the bracket lies in [-1,1]; for k>=2 it lies in [0,1]. Thus 0<=v_k<=a^k<=1 and 1<=Z<=(1+a)^m. For tensors v=v1 tensor v2 and Z=Z1 Z2. R tensorizes and

T=T1+T2,
L=L1 Z2+Z1 L2.

These identities use product-compatible actual phases; no independent zero completion is made. The three-bit factor gives exactly Z=319/256, preserving the archived raw-L guard. The proof uses T throughout and does not propose a raw-L influence payment.

### 3. Direct payment of the named entropy excess

For one factor the exact entropy is

H=-(1-d)^2 log_2((1-d)^2)+4d(1-d/2)(m-1).

The empty contribution is at most 2d/ln2: use -ln(1-d)<=d/(1-d). Since ln2>1/2, this contribution is at most 4d. The remaining contribution is at most 4d(m-1). Hence H<=4md=4I. By the exact cross-entropy identity K=B-H and Gibbs' inequality K>=0, K>=[B-4I]_+. This sums over tensors, and the character extension preserves the claim.

### 4. Scalar entropy cap used for upper collision payment

For 0<=x<=y<=1, writing x=yr,

x log_2(1/x)=yr log_2(1/y)+yr log_2(1/r)
            <=y log_2(1/y)+y/(e ln2).

Apply this with x=v_k, y=a^k and sum multiplicities. Since Z>=1,

T<=L<=ma(1+a)^(m-1) log_2(1/a)+[(1+a)^m-1]/(e ln2).   (U)

All losses in (U) are retained; its all-m application requires the scale bounds in the next section.

### 5. Normalized payment K>=T/4-8I

If 2<=m<=6, H(R)<=m and log_2 Z>=0 give T<=m. Thus T/4<=2^(m-3)I<=8I, while K>=0.

If m=7, d=1/64 and a=63/1024. The exact integer comparison 2*1087^6<3*1024^6 proves (1+a)^6<3/2. Also

(1+a)^7-1 <=7a(1+a)^6.

Using log_2(1/a)<=log_2(1/d)=6 in (U) gives T/4<9I+(3/(2e ln2))I<10I. Here e>8/3 and ln2>2/3 imply e ln2>16/9>3/2. Meanwhile B=7h_2(d)>=6I and H<=4I, so K>=2I. Therefore T/4-K<8I.

If m>=8, the sequences m2^(1-m) and m(m-1)2^(1-m) decrease on this range. Hence

ma<=4md<=1/4,
ma log_2(1/d)<=4m(m-1)d<=7/4.

Since (1+a)^m<=e^(ma)<=1/(1-ma)<=4/3 and e^(ma)-1<=(4/3)ma, (U) gives

T/4 <= I log_2(1/d)+(4/3)I*ma log_2(1/d)+(4/(3e ln2))I
     < I log_2(1/d)+(10/3)I
     <= B+4I.

Combine with K=B-H>=B-4I to obtain T/4-K<=8I. Tensor additivity of K,T,I proves the payment on the entire class.

### 6. Separate recovery B<=T/4+8I

For 2<=m<=7, h_2(d)<=d log_2(1/d)+d/ln2 and 1/ln2<2 imply B<=(m+1)I<=8I; T>=0 suffices.

For m>=8 set s=2(1-d)^(m-1)-1. Bernoulli's inequality gives s>=1-2(m-1)d>0. The singleton collision contribution is v_1=a s^2, with log_2(1/v_1)>=log_2(1/a). All raw entropy contributions are nonnegative. Therefore

T/4 >= I W log_2(1/a),
W=(1-d)s^2/Z >=(1-d)[1-4(m-1)d]e^(-ma)
              >=1-(8m-3)d.

The last step uses e^-x>=1-x and the product of nonnegative (1-u) factors being at least one minus their sum. All factors used here are nonnegative for m>=8. Also log_2(1/a)>=log_2(1/d)-2. Consequently

T/4 >= I [log_2(1/d)-2-(8m-3)d log_2(1/d)].

The sequence (8m-3)(m-1)2^(1-m) decreases for m>=8: its next-term ratio is (8m+5)m/[2(8m-3)(m-1)]<1, since 8m^2-27m+6>0. At m=8 it equals 427/128<7/2. Since B<=I[log_2(1/d)+1/ln2],

B-T/4 <=(2+1/ln2+7/2)I<7I<=8I.

This recovers the marginal entropy budget from the normalized collision statistic with a paid error, independently of the lower-divergence payment. Tensor additivity proves the same recovery bound on C.

### 7. Implication and exact open boundary

For any class on which B<=T/4+cI and K>=T/4-kappa I both hold with the SAME product-marginal reference and phase convention, H=B-K<=(c+kappa)I. This is the sufficient recovery implication. Neither inequality follows from the other. On C the above proof gives c=kappa=8, alongside the sharper direct H<=4I.

First unsupported line for a universal extension: asserting either B<=T/4+8I or K>=T/4-8I for an arbitrary Boolean function. The point-defect formula, its low-energy scale estimates and tensor factorization do not justify either assertion. Support conditioning of a different reference is also not covered. The paired-coordinate conditioned under-recovery control in the archive remains applicable to proposed extensions; it is not contradicted.

# A036 / B08 stage 2: explicit recovery bill and completed-reference guards

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Bits throughout, except ln. Correcting stage 1 notation: M=sum_i h2(Inf_i) is the marginal entropy sum; campaign B=sum_i H(S_i|S_-i) is erasure entropy. TC=D2(P||Q)=M-H(P), where P=fhat^2 and Q is the product of actual spectral marginals. The stage-1 symbol B meant M, not C018's B. Its theorem is unchanged under this renaming.

For a normalized signed reference b, define C_b(U)=sum_S b_S b_{S xor U}, v_U=C_b(U)^2, Z=sum v_U, L=sum v_U log2(1/v_U), and T=L/Z=H(v/Z)-log2 Z. No raw-L payment is proposed. The lead requested exact B05 k=2,4 and addressing a=1,...,4 tests of TC>=T/4-kappa I and M<=T/4+cI, with support terms retained.

The substantive recovery repair is the following universal reference lemma. For ANY finite product law Q with marginals q_i, J=sum q_i<=1/16, M=sum h2(q_i), and ANY sign choices epsilon_S on its support, b_S=epsilon_S sqrt(Q(S)) satisfies

    |T/4-M+(2+log2 e)J| <= 9JM+15J^2.                 (R)

Here J equals physical influence I when Q is the actual spectral-marginal reference. The lemma does not assume Booleanity. It pays recovery with an explicit state-dependent bill; it does not prove divergence payment. In particular, if J,M tend to zero, T/4=M-(2+log2 e)J+o(J).

The six frozen Boolean cases pass kappa=c=8. The first certified zero-price failure in the ordered addressing test is a=4, with T/4-TC>77/100 bits. Addressing with positive zero completion gives the exact limiting coefficient tau described below. This persists on a genuine full-support one-point-flipped addressing family. B05 itself eventually fails zero-price payment with exact limit 8/5-log2 e>0, even though its k=2,4 cases pass. None of these is an unbounded H/I family.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Universal quantitative recovery lemma

Delete q_i=0 coordinates (their spectral bits are forced absent); J<=1/16 excludes q_i=1. The J=0 case is a character-free delta amplitude and T=M=0. For J>0, put E=sum_i q_i log2(1/q_i). Every singleton correlation has the exact formula

    C_b({i})=2sqrt(q_i(1-q_i)) r_i,
    r_i=E_{Q_-i}[epsilon(0,S_-i)epsilon(1,S_-i)].

The all-empty S_-i event has probability at least1-J. Thus |r_i|>=1-2J, and v_i=4q_i alpha_i with

    1-5J <= (1-q_i)(1-2J)^2 <= alpha_i <=1.

The scalar bound -ln alpha<=(1-alpha)/alpha, alpha>=11/16, and 1/ln2<3/2 give log2(1/alpha_i)<=12J. Since log2(1/q_i)-2>=0, the singleton raw entropy obeys

    E-2J-5JE <= L_single/4 <= E-2J+12J^2.             (1)

For any shift U, absolute correlation is bounded by that of the unsigned product amplitude, so v_U<=y_U=prod_{i in U}4q_i. For 0<=x<=y<=1,

    x log2(1/x)<=y log2(1/y)+y/(e ln2).               (2)

Indeed write x=yr and use r log2(1/r)<=1/(e ln2). Summing over |U|>=2,

    sum y_U log2(1/y_U)
      =sum_i 4q_i log2(1/(4q_i)) [prod_{j!=i}(1+4q_j)-1]
      <=4E(e^(4J)-1),
    sum y_U<=e^(4J)-1-4J.

For J<=1/16, e^(4J)-1<=16J/3 and e^(4J)-1-4J<=32J^2/3. These follow from e^x<=1/(1-x) and e^x-1-x<=x^2/[2(1-x)]. With e>8/3 and ln2>2/3, (2) therefore yields

    0<=L_higher/4<=6JE+2J^2.                         (3)

Since v_empty=1, 1<=Z<=prod_i(1+4q_i)<=e^(4J). Combining (1)-(3), and using 1/Z>=1-4J, gives

    E-2J-9JE <= T/4 <= E-2J+6JE+14J^2.              (4)

For the lower bound, E>=4J makes E-2J-5JE nonnegative, so multiplication by 1-4J preserves the required direction. All normalizers are retained.

Finally the convergent identity

    -(1-q)ln(1-q)=q-sum_{l>=2}q^l/[l(l-1)]

gives M=E+(log2 e)J-beta, 0<=beta<=J^2: the tail is at most q^2/[2(1-q)] and 1/ln2<3/2. Substituting into (4), and using E<=M, proves (R). No Boolean cancellation or unproved FEI inequality enters this argument.

The exact first unsupported dimension-free recovery line is replacing 9JM by O(J) without controlling M. The exact first unsupported divergence line is TC>=T/4-kappa I on the general admitted class. The all-phase reference lemma proves neither. On a sequence with I,M->0, however, recovery has now been completely paid with coefficient 2+log2 e+o(1).

### 2. Exact B05 reference and contraction certificate

For even k, let r=2k, p=2^-r, delta=2^-k and s=2^(-k/2). Use b_k(x)=(-1)^(x1*x2+...+x_(k-1)*x_k) on 0/1 physical bits and its one-point flip b'_k. Children G=1+A(z)(b_k-1), H=1+A(z)(b'_k-1), where A indicates all-zero controls. The fresh selector chooses G or H. The actual marginals are

    q_data=p(1/2+delta), q_control=p(1-s+delta), q_selector=p delta.

The parent Fourier coefficients at data T, controls U, selector bit Y are

    Y=0: 1[T=U=empty]+p(c_T-1[T=empty]-delta),
    Y=1: p delta,
    c_T=s*(-1)^(paired quadratic form in T).

All coefficients are nonzero for k=2,4 and for all even k>=2. Thus Q(A)=1, K_supp=0, K_shape=TC, and the reference phase is uniquely actual. Let c_D,c_C,c_Y be the unsigned product amplitudes of the three marginal groups. The same-sign reference has four separable terms:

    b = [s_phase(T)c_D(T)c_C(U)
         -2 c_D(empty)1[T=empty]c_C(U)
         +2 c_D(empty)c_C(empty)1[T=U=empty]]
          *sqrt(1-q_selector)1[Y=0]
        +c_D(T)c_C(U)*sqrt(q_selector)1[Y=1].

Here s_phase is the product of (+,+,+,-) phases on each data pair. For each pair of the four terms, every shifted coordinate contributes a common sqrt(q_i(1-q_i)); every unshifted coordinate contributes a rational q_i or 1-q_i. Consequently C_b(U)^2 is rational even though b itself can contain radicals. The code implements these local tables exactly, then groups shifts by counts of data-pair types 00,01/10,11, control shift weight, and selector shift. Multiplicity is (k/2)!/(e0!e1!e2!)*2^e1*binom(r,l). This is a proved finite contraction, not a numerical radical approximation.

The five spectral classes are the anchor; nonempty-control/empty-data; positive nonempty-data; negative data; and selector-present. Their exact probabilities/multiplicities are in out/verification.json and code/check.py. Integer Walsh transforms of the n=7 and n=13 truth tables independently verify the complete spectra and marginals. A separate direct signed contraction verifies every shift at k=2.

### 3. B05 asymptotic coefficient and zero-price obstruction

As even k->infinity, the nonempty-data Fourier class has total mass p(1+o(1)) and log probability 5k+o(k), so contributes 5kp(1+o(1)) entropy. The nonempty-control/empty-data class has mass p(1+o(1)) and log probability 4k+o(k), contributing 4kp(1+o(1)). Anchor entropy is O(p). Selector mass is p delta, with entropy 3kp delta=o(kp). Therefore

    H/(kp)->9, I/(kp)->5/2, H/I->18/5.

Also I->0 and M=O(k^2 p)->0, directly from the three marginal classes. Apply (R):

    (M-T/4)/I ->2+log2 e,
    (T/4-TC)/I ->18/5-(2+log2 e)=8/5-log2 e.

The latter is in [0.15730495911103,0.15730495911104] by a rational certificate. Thus zero-price lower payment eventually fails on actual full-support B05 functions, while the k=2,4 tests pass it. This is an analytic infinite-family conclusion, not automatic dimension escalation or extrapolation from floating data. It does not refute any fixed kappa greater than this limit.

### 4. Addressing support and three distinct reference conventions

Let N=2^a and F_a(x,y)=y_x. Its only nonzero coefficients are N^-1*(-1)^(V dot j) at (V,{j}); P is uniform on N^2 atoms. Thus

    H=2a, I=1+a/2, M=a+N h2(1/N),
    Q(A)=(1-1/N)^(N-1)=w_N,
    TC=K_supp=-log2 w_N, K_shape=0.

These support terms are not absorbed into a constant without stating them. Conditioned Q(.|A)=P, so the normalized supported amplitude is exactly actual fhat and T=0. A040's subnormalized supported amplitude sqrt(w_N)*fhat instead has Z=w_N^2 and T=-2log2 w_N. Our completed amplitude is normalized on the full product Q: use actual phases on A and +1 phase at every spectral zero. It generally has different Z,T. Completion is specified for each function; no tensor identity for independently completed functions is claimed.

### 5. Completed addressing collision formula

Put t=1/N, a0=4t(1-t), and u=t(1-t)^(N-1). The distinguished data label 0 has constant positive Fourier phase. All other singleton-data phases are address characters. For address shift U and zero data shift,

    C_b(U,0)=1 if U=0; C_b(U,0)=1-Nu if U!=0.

For nonzero data shift V of weight w and e=1[0 in V], put h=w-e. Correlation is independent of U and equals [2sqrt(t(1-t))]^w F_{w,e}, where

    F_{w,e}=1-2^(1-w)[h(1-t)^(N-w)+(N-1-h)t(1-t)^(N-w-1)]
              +1[w=2,e=0]2^(1-w)(1-t)^(N-2).        (5)

Proof: average phases over the address subsets. A singleton with nonzero label paired with a nonsingleton has zero character average; two different singleton labels also have zero average. Remove precisely those pairs from unsigned correlation. When w=2 and neither label is 0, the intersection was subtracted twice; the last term restores it once. For V=0 the same-label character has average (-1)^(U dot j), giving the preceding special formula.

The v groups are therefore 1 at multiplicity1; (1-Nu)^2 at multiplicity N-1; and a0^w F_{w,e}^2 at multiplicity N*binom(N-1,w-e). These formulas include all 2^(a+N) shifts. Direct integer Walsh and separate direct sign contractions verify them at a=1,2; larger cases use the proved formula and exact rational arithmetic.

### 6. Exact addressing slope

For fixed w, the e=0 class dominates as N->infinity. Its limiting F values, including the zero-shift/address-nonzero group, are

    F0=1-e^-1, F1=1-2e^-1, F2=1-e^-1,
    Fw=1-(w+1)/(2^(w-1)e), w>=3.

Here e without subscript denotes Euler's number, not the membership indicator in (5). Let

    z=sum_{w>=0}4^w Fw^2/w! = e^4-12e+30/e-13/e^2,
    z1=sum_{w>=0}w*4^w Fw^2/w! =4e^4-32e+56/e-20/e^2,
    tau=z1/z.

To check the sums, first use Fw_base=1-2(w+1)/(2^w e) at every w. Poisson generating identities give z_base=e^4-12e+20/e and z1_base=4e^4-32e+40/e. Correct w=0 and w=2 to obtain the displayed formulas.

Since |F_{w,e}|<=1 by unsigned-correlation domination and binom(N,w)[4t(1-t)]^w<=4^w/w!, factorial tails dominate both masses and first moments. The e=1 group vanishes for each fixed w. Therefore Z/N->z. In L/(Na), the term w*log2 N/a tends w and contributes z1; the extra phase entropy F^2 log2(1/F^2) is at most1/(e ln2), so its summed contribution tends0 after division by a. The w=0 entropy is bounded and also vanishes on that scale. Consequently

    T/a ->tau, TC->log2 e, M/a->2, I/a->1/2.

Rational intervals give tau in [4.77678254267720,4.77678254267721]. Hence any dimension-independent constants for THIS completed-reference family necessarily satisfy

    kappa >=tau/2 >2.38839127133860,
    c >=4-tau/2 >1.61160872866139.

No finite kappa or c is refuted here; this is an exact coefficient floor, not divergent influence-normalized entropy.

### 7. Reusable stability lemma and transfer to actual full support

For any two unit real reference amplitudes b,c on an n-bit group, let epsilon=||b-c||2 and A=||b||1+||c||1. Young's convolution bound follows from the triangle inequality for translations and gives ||C_b-C_c||2<=A epsilon. Since ||C_b||2<=||b||1 and similarly for c,

    ||v_b-v_c||1 <=A^2 epsilon=:rho.

Both Z are at least1, |Z_b-Z_c|<=rho, and TV(v_b/Z_b,v_c/Z_c)<=rho. If rho<=1/2, a maximal-coupling proof of entropy continuity yields

    |T_b-T_c| <=h2(rho)+n rho+rho/ln2.                (S)

Indeed H(X|Y)<=h2(theta)+theta log2(2^n-1) for coupling error theta; h2 is increasing up to1/2. The log-normalizer difference is at most rho/ln2. The A^2 price is explicit; no dimension-free stability is asserted.

Flip the all-zero physical vertex of F_a, whose value is +1, to -1. With n=a+N and delta=2^(1-n), the new coefficients are fhat'_S=fhat_S-delta. For a>=1, delta<1/N, so every old coefficient keeps its sign and every old zero becomes negative. The new function is Boolean and full Fourier support. Its marginals are

    address: 1/2+delta;
    distinguished data label0: 1/N-delta;
    other data: 1/N+delta.

Thus I'=I+(a+N-2)delta. Compare its actual reference to the original product reference completed NEGATIVELY at zeros. The sign arrays agree exactly and product-coupling gives TV(Q',Q)<=n delta, so epsilon<=sqrt(2n delta). The l1 norms satisfy A<=2sqrt(N)exp(2sqrt(N)) for all sufficiently large a: address factors are at most sqrt2; data marginals are at most2/N and sqrt(q)+sqrt(1-q)<=exp(sqrt(q)). Hence n*A^2*epsilon->0. Formula (S) implies T'-T_negative=o(1).

The negative completion has the same tau limit. After globally negating it, its supported phases are -(-1)^(V dot j) and zero phases are positive. Its V=0 correlations are unchanged. For V!=0, compared with positive completion, only pairings involving the distinguished singleton label0 and a nonsingleton change. For e=0, the extra coefficient is -4*2^-w*t*(1-t)^(N-w-1), tending0 for fixed w; e=1 classes have vanishing weighted multiplicity. The same factorial domination proves unchanged z,z1 and tau.

Physical amplitude distance is sqrt(2delta), so spectral entropy continuity gives H'=H+o(1); binary continuity gives |M'-M|<=n h2(delta)=o(1). Therefore TC'->log2 e, T'/a->tau and the coefficient floors above hold on actual FULL-SUPPORT Boolean functions. This removes a zero-completion-only loophole. Influence is large in this family; no claim is made that the tau/2 floor already applies unchanged to the admitted low-I endpoint.

### 8. Why sparse activation changes the constant ledger

For completeness, sparsely activate the full-support flipped addressing seed with r=ceil(3log2 a) fresh controls and activation probability p=2^-r. Put g=1+A(z)(f'_a-1). Every Fourier coefficient is nonzero: nonempty seed indices have p*fhat'_S on every control subset; empty seed/control-nonempty coefficients are p(mu'-1); the anchor is 1+p(mu'-1)>0 eventually. Here mu'=-delta.

Exact physical influence is p[I'+r(1-mu')]. Since r=o(a), I_g~pa/2->0. The exact activation spectrum gives H_g=p H'+O(rp)~2pa, so H_g/I_g->4. Entropy of a product-marginal reference is at most I_g log2(e(n+r)/I_g), which is O(1/a)->0 because p=Theta(a^-3), n=Theta(2^a). Apply (R), now with the ACTUAL activated reference:

    (T_g/4-TC_g)/I_g ->2-log2 e=0.557304959111... .

Thus the large-I tau/2 floor cannot simply be transported through activation: reference rebalancing changes the statistic. The low-I full-support endpoint still requires kappa>=2-log2 e. This is a scope/normalization audit and reproduces the known FEI-strength admission direction quantitatively, rather than claiming a solution on that endpoint.

## executed validation with domain, arithmetic, commands and outputs

Frozen specification: out/FROZEN_SPEC.json, saved before the six-case pilot. Commands from this run directory: `python -X utf8 code/check.py` and `python -X utf8 code/coefficients.py`. The former checks B05 k=2,4 and addressing a=1,...,4, with the independent Walsh and contraction checks specified above. It uses under60 MB and finishes below the 60-second pilot cap. No heavy job, dimension escalation or GPU was used. Exact outputs include H,M,TC,T,L,Z, support mass and K_supp/K_shape.

Logs use 18 terms of the atanh series, positive ln2 denominator enclosures, exact power-of-two range shifts, and geometric remainder. Internal endpoints are rounded outward to denominator2^90; saved endpoints are rounded outward to the10^-15 rational grid. # A036: normalized occupancy reference through a biased rare-core history

Full FEI remains OPEN. This is a scoped reference-transfer closure, not a new entropy bound for point defects. It consumes the live A039 continuation request and preserves all source, outcome and terminal accounts.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For every integer n>=2 let v_n(z)=1-2*1[z=0] on n independent fair data bits, delta=2^-n, N=n+1. The reached Boolean fixture is F(x,a,b,z)=-1 when x=0,a=1; v_n(z) when x=1,b=1; +1 otherwise. This is A039's actual reached root after the essential u0=0 query; no costs before that reached root are newly paid here. Query a, then one original data coordinate in each branch, stopping with all four physical leaves. Permuting the data bits makes all n^2 adaptive labelled choices equivalent for this core and this reference. No equivalence for general cores is asserted.

P is the squared Fourier law. Write e=P(empty)=(1-delta)^2/4 and zeta=1-e (code uses z for zeta). Complete the nonempty maximum-entropy occupancy reference by M(empty)=e, M(S)=zeta Q_r(S) for S nonempty, r_i=Inf_i/zeta. This M is an explicit normalized law, with the positive-root normalizer retained below. It is distinct from the product reference R=prod Ber(Inf_i). Campaign marginal entropy is sum h2(Inf_i); campaign B is erasure. All logarithms, entropies and divergences are in bits.

Transport this SAME inherited M by erasing each queried Fourier indicator and using the physical common kernel k(y|T)=P_y(T)/(2q(T)), q=(P_0+P_1)/2. On q=0 use k=1/2; propagate every reference row. Do not replace the reference at the intermediate frontier. Let A=D(P||M), E_tot the weighted conditional-erasure KL, O_tot the weighted outcome KL, K_end the mean inherited terminal KL, and TC_end the mean KL to each terminal's actual product projection. The named final conversion excess is

    D_end = TC_end-K_end-E_tot = O_tot+TC_end-A.

Result: D_end<0 for ALL n>=2. More quantitatively D_end<-N*delta/8 for n>=16, and D_end/(N*delta)->-1/2. No terminal surplus withdrawal is needed for this one conversion. The exact terminal entropy and influence are retained; the result does not pay arbitrary incoming debts or prove a universal reference-payment rule.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Actual signed spectra and retained terminals

The root marginals are Inf_x=Inf_a=1/2 and Inf_b=Inf_j=delta/2 for each data j. Its nonzero squared coefficients are two atoms 1/4, two atoms e, and 2^(n+2)-2 atoms delta^2/4. Root Fourier coefficients outside these sectors are zero; M puts reference mass there, and this is retained in A. Defining phi(t)=-t log2 t,

    H(F)=1+2phi(e)+(delta-delta^2/2)(2n+2).

At a=0 the child is the (n+2)-bit point defect on the event x=1,b=1,z=0. The a=1 child has the same squared law translated in the x Fourier indicator. After querying one data bit, two final laws are constant and x-character (entropy zero, influences 0 and 1), and the other two are x-translated versions of the (n+1)-bit point-defect law. Its entropy is

    H_pt=phi((1-delta)^2)+(2delta-delta^2)*2n.

Therefore, with all four reach weights 1/4,

    H_end=H_pt/2,
    I_end=1/2+n*delta/2,
    TC_end=N*h2(delta)/2-H_pt/2,
    H(F)-H_end=2.                                      (2)

The last identity is exact: phi(4e)=4phi(e)-8e cancels all logarithms and all n terms. The inactive character leaf contributes influence 1/4 to the expectation; omitting it gave an initial validation failure, repaired and recorded in out/VALIDATION_REPAIRS.md. No certificate from that failed pilot is adopted.

### 2. Root normalizer, maximum entropy and compatible transport

Let c be the unique positive solution

    1-c=(1-c/(2zeta))^2(1-c*delta/(2zeta))^N.            (3)

Put u=c/(2zeta), t=delta*u. Q_r is the product of two Ber(u) and N Ber(t) bits conditioned on being nonempty; conditioning mass is c. Its conditional marginals are r, so M has the actual root marginals and mass e at empty. Positivity follows since all r_i are interior. The function g(c)=1-c-product(1-cr_i) has g'(0)>0, g(1)<0 and g''(c)<0 on (0,1), giving uniqueness of its positive crossing. The finite code checks separated rational signs at every final root bracket.

For the conditional law, log Q_r(S) is affine in the indicators plus a constant on nonempty S. Every feasible occupancy law has the same expectation of log Q_r. KL nonnegativity thus proves maximum entropy, with no unsupported projection theorem. The complete reference entropy is

    H_M=h2(zeta)+(zeta/c)[2h2(u)+N*h2(t)-h2(c)],
    A=H_M-H(F).                                        (4)

The latter follows from the same moment matching, retaining empty mass and all normalizers. It is checked against directly summed D(P||M) and H(M) in the finite truth-table implementation.

For a single physical query, two KL chain rules under the common kernel give

    D(P||Q)=E+O+mean_y D(P_y||Q_y).

This follows first by conditioning the erased indicator and then by adjoining the common outcome kernel. O=D(fair||Q_Y), Q_y(T)=Q_erased(T)k(y|T)/Q_Y(y). Hence, across this two-query history,

    A=E_tot+O_tot+K_end,
    sigma_end=TC_end-K_end,
    D_end=sigma_end-E_tot=O_tot+TC_end-A.                (5)

No root account is renewed; E_tot is not spent both before and after conversion. The declarations on source-zero fibers are fixed. In this family the erased source laws are positive everywhere at all three queried nodes, proved by the point-defect spectra and independently checked for n2..7. This special fact is not generalized to other cores.

### 3. A feasible law supplies a real reference saving

Set r_x=r_a=1/(2zeta). Select the control pair {x},{a},{x,a} with probabilities 1-r_x,1-r_x,2r_x-1, independently of N Ber(delta/(2zeta)) remaining bits. This law is nonempty and has all the required conditional marginals. Complete it with empty mass e. Its four control-pattern probabilities are e,1/2-e,1/2-e,e, so its completed entropy is

    H_feas=1+h2(2e)+zeta*N*h2(delta/(2zeta)).

Maximum entropy gives H_M>=H_feas; this comparison is only a variational lower bound for the fixed inherited reference, not a reset to the feasible law. With (2)-(5),

    D_end <= O_tot+1-h2(2e)
                     +N[h2(delta)/2-zeta*h2(delta/(2zeta))].

Write h2(s)=s log2(1/s)+s log2(euler_e)-R(s), where

    R(s)=log2(euler_e)*sum_(k>=2) s^k/[k(k-1)].

R(s)/s is increasing. Since delta/(2zeta)<=delta, the bracketed reference saving satisfies

    zeta*h2(delta/(2zeta))-h2(delta)/2
      >= (delta/2)log2(2zeta) > delta/4,                 (6)

because 2zeta>=3/2>sqrt(2), certified by 9>8. No asymptotic inference is used for this inequality.

### 4. Exact outcomes and the all-dimensional bound

Here are the transported outcome normalizers, including all reference mass. Put w=(1-t)^N,

    E=e+(1-u)w/2, X=w/2,
    k=(1-delta/2)^2/[(1-delta/2)^2+delta^2/4],
    q0=1/2+(k-1/2)(E-X), q1=1-q0,
    B0=(delta/2)(1-u)(1-t)^(N-1),
    B1=(t/2)(1-t)^(N-1).

After the first a query and subsequent data erasure, the respective heavy residual reference atoms have masses

    L0=(kE+B0/2)/q0, L1=(kX+B1/2)/q1.

Let kappa=(1-delta)^2/[1+(1-delta)^2]. Each second-query active outcome probability is p_y=1-(1-kappa)L_y. All other residual atoms go to the active outcome with probability one. Thus

    o(q)=-log2(4q(1-q))/2,
    O_tot=o(q0)+[o(p0)+o(p1)]/2.                        (7)

These formulas are independently reconstructed against full reference arrays for n2..7, then used for exact finite bases n8..15 without constructing enormous tables.

For n>=16, N*delta<=17/65536; N*2^-n decreases thereafter. The positive root in u solves G(u)=1-2zeta*u-(1-u)^2(1-delta*u)^N=0. We have G(2/5)>0, u<1/(2zeta)<=2/3. On [2/5,2/3],

    G'(u)<=-3/2+6/5+(9/25)N*delta < -1/4.

Indeed zeta>=3/4 and the two nonnegative product derivative terms are bounded separately. Also |G(1/2)|<=delta/2+N*delta/8. The mean value theorem gives

    |u-1/2| <= (N+4)delta/2,
    1-w <= 2N*delta/3.

Consequently |q0-1/2|<=N*delta/2: use E-X=e-u*w/2, |e-1/4|<=delta/2, and the preceding two estimates. Further

    E>=1/2-(9N/20+3/2)delta>=1/2-N*delta,
    X>=1/2-N*delta,
    k>=1-delta^2.

Both L_y are at least (1/2-2N*delta)/(1/2+N*delta/2)>=1-5N*delta. They are probabilities in [0,1]. Since |kappa-1/2|<=delta, this implies |p_y-1/2|<=6N*delta. All deviations are <=1/10. For |q-1/2|=s<=1/10,

    o(q)<=2*log2(euler_e)*s^2/(1-4s^2)<=4s^2,

using log2(euler_e)<3/2. Hence O_tot<=145*N^2*delta^2. For the reverse fair-law divergence, the second derivative of 1-h2(1/2-s) is 4*log2(euler_e)/(1-4s^2); integrating twice from zero gives the same upper bound 2*log2(euler_e)*s^2/(1-4s^2). Thus 1-h2(2e)<=4delta^2: its deviation from 1/2 is delta-delta^2/2. Applying (6),

    D_end <=150*N^2*delta^2-N*delta/4
          <-N*delta/8, n>=16.                          (8)

The final strict comparison is 1200*17<65536, with monotonic N*delta. This is a genuine all-n bound, not extrapolation from the finite bases. All n2..15 have strictly negative rational upper certificates in out/FORMULA_CHECK.json. Thus D_end<0 for every n>=2.

### 5. Normalized asymptotic price and separate recovery implication

Let R_root be the actual product-marginal law. Because M matches its marginals, Delta=D(M||R_root)=M_root-H_M, with M_root=2+N*h2(delta/2). For nonempty S with k selected controls and l selected remaining bits,

    M(S)/R_root(S) = (1/(2u))*(2u)^k*[2(1-u)]^(2-k)
                   *(2u)^l*[(1-delta*u)/(1-delta/2)]^(N-l).

At empty the ratio is e/[(1/4)(1-delta/2)^N]. Since |u-1/2|=O(N*delta), every such ratio is 1+O(N^2*delta), uniformly in S; expand logarithms on a fixed neighborhood of one and exponentiate, since N^2*delta->0. All ratio factors and the prefactor are displayed, so no support or normalization term is hidden. The elementary log bound KL<=log2(euler_e)*chi-square gives Delta=O(N^4*delta^2)=o(N*delta).

Equations (2),(4),(5) now give exactly

    D_end=O_tot+Delta+N[h2(delta)/2-h2(delta/2)].

O_tot=O(N^2*delta^2), while h2(delta)/2-h2(delta/2)=-delta/2+O(delta^2). Therefore D_end/(N*delta)->-1/2. This is a quantitative normalized divergence-payment margin for the named conversion excess, using a completed occupancy reference; it is distinct from collision T and does not reintroduce raw L.

The recovery implication needed by a downstream reference ledger is proved separately from negativity: from (5), D_end<=0 implies sigma_end<=E_tot, equivalently O_tot+TC_end<=A. Adding the SAME full H_end yields

    O_tot+TC_end+H_end <= A+H_end.

This retains all terminal bills; the final conversion is paid by the single original source account with its erasure release, and no final child surplus is withdrawn. The inherited K_end is the debt being converted, not an additional reserve. None of these inequalities pays H_end universally by influence or licenses treating KL as a new entropy-payment bank.

### 6. Exact first unsupported line

For an arbitrary non-flat core, the root feasible law still exists, but identity H(root)-H_end=2, the heavy-atom kernel, and the negative saving domination need not hold. The first unsupported universal line is: choose an actual mixed policy with O_tot+TC_end-A and the other incoming owned debts paid by previously unused source/child accounts while retaining every terminal reference bill. Priority-separator costs preceding this reached root remain separately owned. This stage neither assumes universal 4I-H>=0 nor spends a renewed occupancy projection. The shortest continuation is a core whose two data children are both nonconstant and unequal, where the exact entropy decrement changes and the same source reference must still be retained.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

PROOF.md gives the full derivation, root normalizer existence/uniqueness, affine-moment matching, separate signed recovery equivalence, all-k margin, hereditary terminal certificates and conservative-rule counterfamily. The source reference is Q(empty)=1/16 and Q(s)=(15/(16c)) product_i(cr_i)^si(1-cr_i)^(1-si) on nonempty s, where r_i=Inf_i/(15/16), and c is the unique positive root of1-c=product(1-cr_i). # A037: conditional posterior credit ledger

Full uniform-cube FEI remains OPEN. This run closes the finite-hierarchy bookkeeping target on an explicitly common probability space. Author proofs and exact validation are supplied; independent review is pending. No novelty claim is made for Shannon/KL chain rules.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

All information is in bits. A tree is finite, with adaptive choices measurable from its history and any declared common random seed. W is one fixed finite witness variable; A is optional finite side information (for example the Fourier set being described). Every node posterior is a conditional law of this SAME W under ONE joint probability measure. Node h has probability p_h, next outcome Y_h, and children hy. Zero-probability nodes are omitted. Leaf L includes the full stopped transcript, so variable depths introduce no extra unrecorded stop symbol. A randomized stop is itself a recorded outcome. Entropy conservation needs no reference law. KL conservation uses finite laws P,Q with P absolutely continuous with respect to Q on all used atoms, and otherwise holds in extended values with no infinity subtraction. The formulas below are stated for finite divergences.

Two distinct resources are retained: initial conditional witness entropy H_P(W|A), and initial divergence D_2(P_W||Q_W). They are not interchangeable, nor are independently optimized child witnesses automatically posteriors of one root witness.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### E. Conditional entropy release, stopping and no reuse

Define the release at h by c_h=I_P(W;Y_h|A,H=h), with the conditioning on A averaged under its law at h. Equivalently,

    c_h = H_P(W|A,H=h) - sum_y P(y|h) H_P(W|A,H=hy).

The entropy in the child term includes the updated distribution of A: this is essential. To verify the formula, expand each entropy into sums over a and use P(a,y|h)=P(y|h)P(a|hy). For each a the conditional mutual information is a weighted KL between P(W|a,hy) and P(W|a,h); Gibbs makes c_h nonnegative. Multiply by p_h. Every internal entropy term cancels its occurrence as a child, yielding

    sum_{h internal} p_h c_h + sum_{l leaf} p_l H_P(W|A,L=l)
      = H_P(W|A).

Thus total released information equals I_P(W;L|A) and is at most the initial resource. The terminal term is generally positive. The same identity applied below any positive-probability h bounds all future release by H_P(W|A,H=h). A previously revealed bit that is measurable from A,h has zero future release. These are expected conditional statements; individual realized surprisals need not decrease.

For any cut/frontier F separating root from all leaves, the root identity splits into release above F plus the expected remaining conditional entropy at F, and each F-node resource splits again below F. This proves conditioning cannot recharge the root account. If several credits concern coordinates or labels of W, expose them in a single joint order conditioned on earlier exposures. Adding separate marginal informations is invalid when they overlap. Example: W is a fair bit, reveal W twice. Root release is 1 bit; second release is 0. Recharging H(W)=1 at the second step wrongly charges 2 bits.

Side conditioning cannot be dropped: if W,L are independent fair bits and A=W XOR L, then I(W;L)=0 but I(W;L|A)=1. A conditional witness ledger must consistently keep the Fourier set A and all previous reveals.

For the forest residual in the plan, take P(A,r)=P_f(A|A nonempty) P_A(r), with r ranging over distinct actual successful finite runs. If r is finite-valued (or has finite entropy with a justified countable limiting argument), the entropy rebate decomposes exactly as

    H_P(r|A)=I_P(r;L|A)+H_P(r|A,L).

Consequently one may replace the original rebate by released credit PLUS the terminal rebate; one cannot subtract the original rebate and also subtract its released part. The spectral weighting is v=P_f(A nonempty), so the entire identity is multiplied by v in the complete residual. The initial term v(Lambda+log2 Z), gate costs and chosen scale-prior costs remain separate. This result does not bound any of them by influence.

### K. Divergence ledger and the exact corrections

Initially omit A; the same proof holds conditional on each a and averaging. Let p_h(w),q_h(w) be the node posteriors under P,Q. Let k_h^P(y|w), k_h^Q(y|w) be the reveal channels. Put

    K_h = D_2(p_h||q_h),
    d_h = D_2(P(Y_h|h)||Q(Y_h|h)),
    j_h = sum_w p_h(w) D_2(k_h^P(.|w)||k_h^Q(.|w)).

Expanding the joint divergence two ways proves

    K_h+j_h = d_h+sum_y P(y|h)K_hy.

Indeed the likelihood ratio factors first as (p_h/q_h)(k_h^P/k_h^Q), and second as the outcome likelihood ratio times the Bayes-posterior ratio. Sum under the joint P(w,y|h). Multiplying by p_h and telescoping gives

    sum_h p_h d_h + sum_l p_l K_l
      = K_root + sum_h p_h j_h.

All d_h,j_h,K_l are nonnegative. With a single common channel k_h^P=k_h^Q at every node, the injection is zero: total released KL plus terminal KL is exactly the initial divergence. Below a node h, the available KL is K_h, not the original K_root. Replaying an already recorded outcome has d_h=0. Different channels are permitted only with the displayed injection; it is an additional unpaid resource, not free root credit.

This does NOT say posterior KL K_h decreases pathwise. For a common informative channel, the P-expected posterior KL decreases after paying d_h. By contrast D(P(W|h)||P(W)) generally grows as observations accumulate; it is earned posterior information, not a remaining KL reserve. These two directions must not be confused.

To compare freely reset node reference laws r_h, compute the Bayes-predicted child reference q_hy from r_h and the selected Q-channel. Define the signed reset correction

    delta_hy = sum_w p_hy(w) log2(q_hy(w)/r_hy(w)).

Then D(p_hy||r_hy)=D(p_hy||q_hy)+delta_hy, and the local reset ledger is

    D(p_h||r_h)+j_h+sum_y P(y|h)delta_hy
      = d_h+sum_y P(y|h)D(p_hy||r_hy).

The cumulative formula has the same signed reset terms weighted by child visit probabilities. Dropping them invalidates the conserved-resource claim. Infinite/support-changing resets require support checks before subtraction. Common subprobability references should be completed by a failure atom (P gives it zero mass), or normalized with their total mass logged explicitly. Normalizing different node masses silently changes the ledger.

An extreme exact control: W=Y is fair under P, but Q makes W,Y independent and fair. Root K=0; release d=0; expected terminal posterior K=1, and channel injection j=1. The missing bit comes entirely from using different channels. With a common reveal Y=W, the posteriors are point masses under both laws, and replaying Y gives zero release. Resetting the reference to uniform before every replay injects a bit each time through delta; there is no conserved root bound after omitting those resets.

### P. A common lift of actual adaptive physical restriction spectra

This provides an explicit joint probability space, not an assumed stochastic map uniform over functions. At a tree node with Boolean restricted function g on R, choose a physical block B determined by the physical history; let C=R\B. Write

    a_U,T = ghat(U union T),
    b_y,T = sum_{U subset B} a_U,T chi_U(y) = (g|_{B=y})hat(T),
    q_T = sum_U a_U,T^2 = 2^(-|B|) sum_y b_y,T^2.

Draw the root spectral set S_0 with law ghat(S)^2. At each node, current S_h is S_0 intersect R. Given its C-part T, choose physical outcome y with

    k_h(y|S_h) = 2^(-|B|) b_y,T^2/q_T

when q_T>0; a zero fiber is unreachable and can be assigned any normalized kernel. Then set S_hy=T. Equivalently at a node the joint atom is

    P(U,T,y|h)=a_U,T^2 * 2^(-|B|) b_y,T^2/q_T.

Parseval gives sum_y k_h=1. Summing U gives P(y,T|h)=2^(-|B|)b_y,T^2; summing T gives P(y|h)=2^(-|B|) because each child is Boolean. Thus the physical branch probabilities are precisely fair, and P(S_hy=T|hy)=b_y,T^2, the actual child spectrum. Inductively the construction supplies one finite common joint space for every finite adaptive physical block tree, with no rerevealed physical coordinates.

At each node it also imposes U independent of Y given T. Hence I(S_h;Y|h)=I(T;Y|h), the local physical-child mixture information. The root-information decomposition is

    I(S_0;Y|h)=I(T;Y|h)+I(S_0\R;Y|T,h).

The second term is zero in this lift: k_h depends only on T,h, so Y is conditionally independent of all the rest of S_0 given T,h. Therefore the cumulative physical mixture information equals I(S_0;L), with terminal remainder H(S_0|L). This proves a common-space conservation law for mixture information on adaptive physical trees. It does not identify the terminal root posterior with the leaf Fourier entropy: when all coordinates are fixed, S_h is empty but S_0 can remain uncertain. The full entropy decrement also includes erased-label conditional entropy, not just mixture information.

More precisely let B_h=H(S_h intersect B|S_h intersect C,H=h) and J_h=I(T;Y|h). Parent entropy is H(T|h)+B_h and the expected child entropy is H(T|Y,h), proving the local decrement B_h+J_h. Telescope this identity and substitute sum_h p_h J_h=I(S_0;L) to obtain the exact residual identification

    H(S_0|L) = sum_h p_h B_h + sum_l p_l H(f_l).

Thus the common-space terminal posterior is precisely cumulative unpaid erasure plus terminal spectral entropy. This is a useful compatibility identity, not an estimate of that residual. The existing block-information bound J_h<=(2/ln 2) I_B(f_h), together with influence conservation, already pays sum p_h J_h by (2/ln 2) I(f); it does not pay the right side above. No new FEI conclusion follows by bounding the mixture information again.

The lift uses Fourier phases through b_y,T. A universal phase-blind channel on parent squared spectra is impossible. On two bits (bits 0,1 represented by signs (-1)^x), take f=[1,1,1,-1] and g=x_1 f, so g=[1,1,-1,1]. Both parent spectra are uniform on the four subsets. Restrict bit 0 to index value 0 (sign +1): f's child is constant and g's child is the bit-1 character. Identical parent squared laws thus give different actual named child laws. The constructed kernel is function-specific; it does not justify applying data processing to a reoptimized convolution reference unless the same kernel or the correction in K is proved.

An arbitrary root witness r can be appended via its chosen conditional law given S_0 and then propagated using this lift. Its descendants are well-defined posteriors. They need not equal independently selected witness laws for the restricted functions. A transfer correction or compatible construction is still needed. This is the exact first unsupported line in any proposed universal FEI application: the claim that child reoptimization/reference replacement costs and the remaining erased-label/witness cost admit one dimension-independent influence payment.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Bits throughout. Physical inputs are independent fair bits indexed x0,x1,z0,...,z_(r-1),y. Let b(x)=(-1)^(x0*x1), b'(x)=b(x)-2*1[x=0]. The selector core g(x,y) is b for y=0 and b' for y=1. Set F_r=1+1[z=0](g-1). The principal finite certificate is r=4, a genuine seven-bit Boolean function. These are specified orders, not optimized trees. Every node's posterior, reset and terminal account is retained in out/EXACT_LEDGER.json.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Compensated identity and common reference convention

Parent residual law q(T) is the average of actual signed-transform child squared laws p0(T),p1(T). Hence H(f)=B_h+J_h+average H(child). The marginal influences average, giving M(f)=D_h+G_h+average M(child). Subtract and use B_h=D_h-A_h:

    TC(f_h)=A_h-J_h+G_h+average TC(child).

Summing with physical weights w_h gives exactly

    sum w_h A_h + sum w_h G_h + E TC(leaf)
      = TC(root)+sum w_h J_h,
    sum w_h(D_h-A_h)+E H(leaf)=H(root)-sum w_h J_h.

These are the existing compensated identities, independently reconstructed here for the selected application; they are not claimed as a new theorem. They distinguish correlation credit A_h from the physical-child mixture credit J_h.

For a direct compatible reference check, let Q(T) be the product of the parent's residual spectral marginals. Use k(y|T)=p_y(T)/(2q(T)) on positive q fibers and k=1/2 on zero fibers for BOTH P and Q. Then Q_Y(y)=sum_T Q(T)k(y|T), and Bayes-predicted child reference Q_y(T)=Q(T)k(y|T)/Q_Y(y). Let R_y be the product of the actual child marginals. The signed reset is delta_y=E_(p_y) log2(Q_y/R_y). Its exact identity is

    average delta_y = D_2(fair||Q_Y)+J_h-G_h.

The code computes delta directly from rational Q_y,R_y, rather than defining it by the right side. Channel injection is zero under this common-kernel convention. Zero-fiber extensions may change D_out and delta separately; the convention is frozen and their compensated difference is invariant. A negative reset is a saving relative to the transported reference, not an independent newly minted bank.

### 2. Exact r=4 tree ledger

The root has I=19/64. Each displayed number below is a rounded illustration of an exact rational prime-log vector saved in out/EXACT_LEDGER.json. Those vectors, not these decimals, certify the identities. The interval certificates separately prove the stated comparisons.

| Tree | sum D | sum A | sum Gamma | sum JS | E TC(leaf) | E H(leaf) | sum B + E H(leaf) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| CF-core | .790760418 | .318701339 | .811065157 | .397939178 | .013557324 | .138557324 | .610616403 |
| CF-split | .841465300 | .327648581 | .849974922 | .432238862 | 0 | .0625 | .576316719 |
| SF-split | .887845102 | .371399951 | .803595121 | .429610429 | 0 | .0625 | .578945151 |
| CF-full | .872715300 | .327648581 | .881224922 | .463488862 | 0 | 0 | .545066719 |
| SF-full | .919095102 | .371399951 | .834845121 | .460860429 | 0 | 0 | .547695151 |

Root H is approximately 1.008555581 and root TC approximately .745384642. CF-split and SF-split have the same complete leaf spectra and expected leaf entropy 1/16, but different intermediate erasure/JS allocations. Certified SF-split minus CF-split net bill lies strictly between 26/10000 and 27/10000 bits. Appending the same data suffix subtracts 1/32 bit from both net bills, so the full-tree gap is identical. Each of the five retained-terminal net bills is certified below 8I; this is calibration, not a new family FEI bound (A023 already paid it).

The reset totals for CF-core, CF-split, SF-split are approximately -.353418234, -.357575204, -.363763338; all are certified negative. Their respective sum D_out values are .059707745, .060160856, .010221353. The different reset decompositions cannot be added across the two trees. Either complete ledger uses its own single sequence of inherited references.

### 3. Which node class owns the unpaid term?

CF-split weighted control erasure is .472059079, selector erasure .041757640, terminal entropy .0625. SF-split weighted control erasure is .462076744, root-selector erasure .054368408, terminal entropy .0625. The control chain carries most of the retained erasure. Their Gamma values are approximately .258497,.435011,.722494,1.235515. Inactive control branches are constants. The remaining active selector core carries the saved terminal account at weight 1/16; after selector exposure the b branch retains two bits of entropy at weight 1/32, and the b' character retains zero. This names actual node/terminal owners instead of calling all correlation KL a fungible bank.

### 4. All-rarity quadratic cancellation for this fixed core

The core's Fourier coefficients are seven atoms of magnitude 1/4 (including empty) and one coefficient -3/4. Thus beta=E g=1/4, c=1-beta=3/4, I(g)=7/4, H(g)=4-(9/8)log2 3, M(g)=6-(9/4)log2 3, TC(g)=2-(9/8)log2 3. Put p=2^-r. The live control node with m unexposed controls is F_m and is reached with weight p*2^m; its control influence is c*2^-m and core-coordinate influences are (3/4,3/4,1/4)*2^-m.

Consequently the exact marginal control charge is

    D_ctrl(r)=p sum_(m=1)^r 2^m h2(c*2^-m).

Set phi(t)=h2(t)-h2(2t)/2. The exact reset-transfer sum is

    G_ctrl(r)=p sum_(m=1)^r 2^m [ (m-1)phi(c*2^-m)
                                  +2phi((3/4)*2^-m)+phi((1/4)*2^-m) ],
    D_ctrl(r)+G_ctrl(r)+p M(g)=M(F_r).

No independent reserve is created by this equality: it is the marginal-entropy telescope on the same physical path.

For 0<t<1, expand -ln(1-t) to obtain

    h2(t)=t log2(1/t)+t log2(e)-R(t),
    0<=R(t)<=t^2/[2(1-t) ln2].

Since t<=3/8 along these live controls and sum_(m>=1)2^-m=1,

    D_ctrl(r)/p=(3/8)r(r+1)+(3/4)r log2(4e/3)+epsilon_r,
    -9/(20 ln2)<=epsilon_r<=0.

This explicitly proves a quadratic marginal bill. Also M(F_r)=(r+2)h2(3p/4)+h2(p/4)=(3/4)p r^2+O(pr). Therefore G_ctrl(r)=(3/8)p r^2+O(pr), with constants depending only on this fixed core.

For completeness the exact root entropy comes from the empty atom (1-3p/4)^2, 2^r-1 control-only atoms (3p/4)^2, and 2^r copies of each nonempty core atom p^2*ghat(T)^2:

    H(F_r)=-(1-3p/4)^2 log2((1-3p/4)^2)
           +(3p-9p^2/8)r
           +p[6-(9/4)log2 3]-p^2[9/4-(9/8)log2 3].

In particular H(F_r)<=3pr+p[M(g)+(3/2)log2(e)]. The empty-atom bound uses -u log2 u<=(1-u)log2(e), and the other coefficients are explicit. Since 0<=B_ctrl<=H(F_r)-p H(g), the released correlation sum A_ctrl=D_ctrl-B_ctrl obeys

    A_ctrl(r)=(3/8)p r^2+O(pr).

The physical influence is I(F_r)=p(7+3r)/4. Hence D_ctrl/I, A_ctrl/I and G_ctrl/I each grow as r/2+O(1), while the retained-terminal erasure bill B_ctrl+p H(g) is O(I). The resource TC(F_r) itself has a quadratic leading term. This is a quantitative explanation of why these accounts can each be much larger than influence and still cancel in one owned ledger. It does not license spending their magnitudes separately. It is confined to this fixed core; no claim is made about arbitrary overlapping controls or core witnesses.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Canonical reference and a rigorous rational implementation

Let P be the raw squared Fourier law, v=1-P(empty), d_j=P(j in S) and r_j=d_j/v. The canonical entropy maximizer on nonempty sets has theta_j=c r_j, where the unique positive c satisfies

    1-c=product_j(1-c r_j).

Completing it by the original empty mass gives M(empty)=P(empty), M(S)=v product_j theta_j^(1[j in S])(1-theta_j)^(1[j not in S])/c for nonempty S. Log M on the nonempty support is affine in its indicators; matching marginals and Gibbs prove the entropy-projection property. The polynomial is strictly concave over the supported interior, vanishes at zero, has positive slope there and is negative at one, giving the unique positive crossing. The independent checker obtains a 48-step Fraction bracket [c_lo,c_hi] and verifies both endpoint signs.

The actual calculation uses a single EXACT RATIONAL reference M0. Set c0=(c_lo+c_hi)/2, theta0=c0*r, Z0=1-product_j(1-theta0_j), and normalize the nonempty product by Z0, not by c0. Preserve M0(empty)=P(empty). This avoids pretending that a rational approximation satisfies the canonical normalization equation. M0 is chosen once at the root and is retained without any descendant selection. Its normalized mass is checked exactly.

For every root atom the checker bounds the canonical M(S) by rational products at the two bracket endpoints, including the reciprocal c normalizer. It then certifies

    abs(log2(M(S)/M0(S))) < epsilon = 2^-36.

This supplies an explicit approximation error rather than silently substituting a different reference law.

### 2. Why the reference approximation is paid once, independently of depth

If two root references obey 2^-epsilon Q0<=Q<=2^epsilon Q0 atomwise, every common nonnegative stochastic kernel preserves that inequality on the JOINT terminal-history/output law. This follows by multiplying each root atom by the same kernel and summing; no KL or entropy estimate is assumed. Erasure of a spectral coordinate is such a marginal map. The function-specific physical kernel is likewise common to both references, including a declared fair extension on source-zero rows.

At a positive history l, each conditional reference divides the joint atom by its own history mass. The two masses obey the same epsilon ratio bound, hence posterior references satisfy a 2epsilon ratio bound. This depends on conditioning ONCE on the complete history, not on charging the error at each intermediate normalization. Therefore root KL A differs by at most epsilon, cumulative outcome KL O_tot (a KL to the complete transcript reference) by at most epsilon, and expected terminal KL by at most 2epsilon. In particular

    abs(D_end(M)-D_end(M0)) <= 2epsilon.

For the conditional-versus-quotient decomposition the error is at most 4epsilon because it subtracts full and quotient reference KLs. These bounds hold for any finite compatible tree length; they do not apply to newly reselected descendant models. This is the precise approximation/no-reuse interface used in the certificate.

### 3. Exact physical transitions and complete account

For queried i, q(T)=P(T)+P(T union i)=(P0(T)+P1(T))/2, with the children obtained from the signed raw Walsh transform. Erase i in Q as well. Apply k(y|T)=P_y(T)/(2q(T)) on positive fibers and k=1/2 otherwise to both actual and reference residual laws. This gives Q_Y and Bayes-normalized child Q_y. Exact likelihood-ratio factorization on every supported atom proves

    D(P||Q)=E_i+O_i+average_y D(P_y||Q_y).

Iterating with true physical weights gives A=E_tot+O_tot+K_end. The sole final reset to child products has sigma=TC_end-K_end and D_end=O_tot+TC_end-A. Thus D_end<0 means the original account pays the full final outcome and product-KL obligations; it does not discard the terminal spectral entropy, and it does not certify any upstream controller/core cost.

Let T=(x,b) at a final leaf and Z its remaining data coordinate(s). The code separately retains sigma_T=D(P_T||R_T)-D(P_T||Q_T) and sigma_Z=[D(P||R)-D(P_T||R_T)]-[D(P||Q)-D(P_T||Q_T)]. Their sum is sigma. A positive quotient component cannot be erased merely because the conditional component saves more. Neither component is spent separately from the complete reset.

    D_end < -19/100,
    conditional sigma > 1/4.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Source I-projection and exact change of account

For mu=sum r_i=1 every mass is on a singleton, and that law is uniquely fixed by r. For mu>1, the source equation 1-c=prod(1-c r_i) makes independent Bernoulli(c r_i) nonempty with probability c. Conditioning yields marginals r. On nonempty subsets log Q_r is affine in the coordinate indicators, after deleting forced coordinates. Therefore E_P+ log Q_r=E_Q_r log Q_r. Gibbs gives Hmax(r)-H(P+)=D(P+||Q_r)>=0. This justifies the exact source formula; no arbitrary sign-free law is assumed Boolean.

P_all and M have identical empty mass 1-v and coordinate marginals d_i=v r_i. On nonempty sets,
log(M/Q_d)=log v+log Q_r-log Q_d,
an affine function of the coordinate indicators plus a constant. On the empty atom its expectation is fixed by 1-v. Consequently
E_P_all log(M/Q_d)=E_M log(M/Q_d).
Expanding relative entropy gives
D(P_all||Q_d)=D(P_all||M)+D(M||Q_d).
The first term is v D(P+||Q_r)=v K_occ. The second is Delta>=0.

Equivalently, cross entropy against Q_d is sum_i h_2(d_i), while
H(M)=h_2(v)+v Hmax(r).
This proves the displayed explicit Delta formula. It is the additional entropy gap of the full-law product reference after the nonempty maximum-entropy step; it is not conditional occupancy KL.

Let c_d=Q_d(nonempty) and Q_d+=Q_d(.|nonempty). KL chain rule on the empty/nonempty event gives the additional exact formula
Delta=D_2(Bern(v)||Bern(c_d))+v D_2(Q_r||Q_d+).
The inner Pythagorean identity follows because log(Q_r/Q_d+) is affine on nonempty subsets. This separates event normalization from nonempty-reference tilt. Both terms are nonnegative and may be large. Zero-probability endpoints use the forced support without infinity subtraction.

### 2. Actual point defects have K_occ exactly zero

For every integer m>=2 on m uniform physical bits, take
f_m(x)=1-2*1_{x=0}, d=2^(1-m), N=2^m.
The Walsh amplitudes are 1-d at empty and -d at every nonempty set. Thus
P_all(empty)=(1-d)^2, P_all(S)=d^2 for S nonempty,
v=2d-d^2, d_i=d, I=md,
P+(S)=1/(N-1), r_i=d/v=1/(2-d).

Take c=1-1/N=1-d/2. Then c r_i=1/2 and 1-c=(1/2)^m. The source I-projection Q_r is the uniform law on all nonempty subsets, exactly P+. Therefore
K_occ(f_m)=0 for every m>=2,
M=P_all, Delta=K_full.

A direct entropy estimate gives H(f_m)<=4I. Indeed the empty contribution
-2(1-d)^2 log2(1-d)<=2d/ln2<4d,
using -ln(1-d)<=d/(1-d) and ln2>1/2. The nonempty contribution equals
4d(1-d/2)(m-1)<=4d(m-1).
Hence
Delta=K_full=m h_2(d)-H(f_m)>=(m-5)I,
because h_2(d)>=d log2(1/d)=d(m-1).
This proves that Delta/I diverges, despite K_occ=0 and I->0. A036's full-support and low-influence hypotheses do not remove this distinction. This is a normalization audit, not a Shannon FEI counterexample: the family still has H/I<=4.

### 3. The proposed normalized collision substitution fails for every fixed coefficient

Use precisely A036's full-support phase convention:
Q_d(S)=d^|S|(1-d)^(m-|S|),
b_empty=+sqrt(Q_d(empty)), b_S=-sqrt(Q_d(S)) for S nonempty.
Let C_b(U)=sum_S b_S b_(S xor U), w_U=C_b(U)^2,
Z=sum_U w_U, T=Z^(-1)sum_U w_U log2(1/w_U).
The temporary symbol w here avoids confusing collision masses with spectral variance v.

Set a=4d(1-d). For a nonempty shift of size k,
C_b(U)=(2 sqrt(d(1-d)))^k [1-2^(2-k)(1-d)^(m-k)],
w_k=a^k [1-2^(2-k)(1-d)^(m-k)]^2.
Proof: the all-positive product amplitude has autocorrelation (2 sqrt(d(1-d)))^k. Changing the sign only at the empty coefficient subtracts 4 sqrt(Q_d(empty)Q_d(U)), giving the formula. This independently reconstructs A036's formula.

For k=1 the bracket lies in [-1,1]; for k>=2 it lies in [0,1]. Thus 0<=w_k<=a^k<=1, so every entropy summand is nonnegative, w_0=1 and 1<=Z<=(1+a)^m. For m>=8 put s=2(1-d)^(m-1)-1>0. Bernoulli gives s>=1-2(m-1)d, and the singleton mass is w_1=a s^2. Keeping only the m singleton contributions yields
T/(4I)>=(1-d)s^2/Z * log2(1/a).
The prefactor W=(1-d)s^2/Z satisfies
W>=(1-d)[1-4(m-1)d]e^(-ma)
 >=(1-d)[1-4(m-1)d][1-4md]
 >=1-(8m-3)d.
Every factor used here is nonnegative for m>=8. Also log2(1/a)>=m-3. The sequence (8m-3)(m-1)2^(1-m) decreases on m>=8: its next-term ratio is (8m+5)m/[2(8m-3)(m-1)]<1 since 8m^2-27m+6>0. At m=8 it is 427/128<4. Therefore
T/(4I)>=(m-3)-(8m-3)d(m-3)
 >=m-3-427/128>m-7.

For every fixed finite kappa choose integer m>=8 with m>kappa+7. Then T/4-kappa I>0, whereas K_occ=v K_occ=0. This is an all-dimensional analytic counterfamily to the explicitly frozen cross-law payment candidate. No tensor amplification, floating-point sign or automated dimension escalation is needed.

The m=16 finite certificate has I=1/2048, v=65535/1073741824,
12.953250698869 <= T/(4I) <= 12.953250698870,
12.512401622344 <= K_full/I <= 12.512401622345.
Thus the substituted kappa=8 payment fails by more than 4.95325 I, while the original A036 K_full payment is unaffected.

### 4. A controlled balanced lift explains why balanced debt is different

Let F_m(z,x)=z f_m(x) with a fresh physical character z. Its spectrum is the spectrum of f_m with z appended to every set; F_m is balanced, H(F_m)=H(f_m), I(F_m)=1+md. The occupancy marginal for z is 1. Hence the source I-projection is simply z appended to Q_d and
K_occ(F_m)=K_full(f_m), Delta(F_m)=0.
Indeed the root variance is now 1, all reference sets contain z and the nonempty conditioning disappears. The same underlying defect can therefore have zero K_occ before balancing and large K_occ after balancing. Neither normalization may be silently carried across an actual transformation.

On this specific lifted family the already established H(f_m)<=4md gives the explicit canonical balanced debt payment
K_occ(F_m)>= [Hmax(r(F_m))-4(I(F_m)-1)]_+.
This is an audit application of the known point-defect entropy estimate, not a new class FEI theorem. It does not justify the same inequality for arbitrary balanced seeds.

### 5. Surviving gap and correction scope

The imported antipodal two-flip dictator reserve no-go concerns D_Gamma-lambda v K_occ with positive fixed lambda; our no-substitution obstruction concerns the opposite task of making conditional K_occ pay an unconditioned collision debt. They are distinct failures. The exact correction Delta neither proposes such a reserve nor solves the canonical debt. No new estimate is asserted for arbitrary prime gates, physical-child reference resets, or overlapping composition.

First unsupported universal line: proving
K_occ>=[Hmax(r)+a_q-sum r_i a_i]_+
for arbitrary source-realizable prime gates at all required biases, or proving a nonreusing physical transfer of the exact state-dependent Delta together with this occupancy debt. Positivity of Delta and K_occ does not pay either. The balanced slice remains FEI-strength.

# A038 stage 2: exact release of point-defect normalization credit

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Follow B08 lead notes v2: determine how much of root Delta is released as Gamma versus occupancy mutual-information credit along a physical coordinate tree of a point defect. This stage resolves that scoped mathematical target analytically with explicit error bounds, and prices the actual Bayes-reference reset. Full FEI remains OPEN.

For every integer n>=2, let f_n=1-2*1_{x=0} on the uniform physical cube. At every nonconstant node query an unqueried physical coordinate, with adaptive choices allowed, stopping at constant leaves. Physical translations and a global output sign give the same squared laws. All such complete singleton trees have the same ledger; no choice of coordinate order is assumed favorable. Proper-block queries and stopping at paid nonconstant leaves are outside the no-go scope.

Information is in bits; L=log2(e). Use the lead's notation M=sum_i h2(Inf_i), TC=M-H, Gamma_i=sum_{j!=i}[h2(Inf_j(parent))-average h2(Inf_j(child))]. Credit C_h=I(S_i;S_-i) is mutual information, distinct from physical total influence I. For point defects, source-exact K_occ=0 at every active node and Delta=TC. Constant leaves have Delta=TC=0. The k=1 character endpoint has zero spectral entropy and all information charges zero.

Main quantified results: let d=2^(1-n), I_n=nd and weighted tree sums G_n=sum w_h Gamma_h, C_n=sum w_h C_h, J_n=sum w_h JS_h. Then
Delta_n+J_n=G_n+C_n,
G_n=n h2(d)-sum_{k=1}^n 2^(k-n)h2(2^(1-k)).
For A_n=n(n-1)/2,
A_n d <=G_n<=(A_n+2L)d,
[2(n-1)-1]d<=J_n<=[2(n-1)+4L-1]d.
Consequently G_n/Delta_n and C_n/Delta_n tend to 1/2, J_n/Delta_n tends to zero. Ratios to Delta are used only for n>=3; Delta_2=0.

With the source-compatible coherent reveal kernel applied under both P and its product reference, the accumulated Bayes-outcome KL O_n and signed reference-reset correction R_n satisfy
0<=O_n<=8L d,
R_n=O_n+J_n-G_n, R_n/Delta_n -> -1/2.
The source kernel has no channel injection. Its signed reference reset cannot be discarded. Accumulated Gamma is not O(I) on ANY full singleton coordinate tree of these sources, since G_n/I_n>=(n-1)/2. This is not a statement about arbitrary proper-block rules or FEI failure.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

The full derivation, including all endpoint and inequality details, is in DERIVATION.md. The central calculation is as follows.

At the unique active node with k coordinates left, let d_k=2^(1-k). Its visit probability is w_k=2^(k-n), so w_k d_k=d. The next physical outcomes are a constant and a (k-1)-bit point defect, each with probability 1/2. Write phi(t)=h2(t)-h2(2t)/2, q0_k=(1-d_k)^2+d_k^2 and beta_k=2d_k(1-d_k). Directly from the actual Walsh law,
Gamma_k=(k-1)phi(d_k),
JS_k=phi(beta_k),
B_k=q0_k h2(d_k^2/q0_k)+beta_k,
C_k=h2(d_k)-B_k.
The exact nonempty residual-fiber contribution is beta_k; its undercount in the initial draft was caught by the first exact checker and corrected before any successful certificate.

With H_k the exact source entropy and Delta_k=k h2(d_k)-H_k,
H_k=B_k+JS_k+H_(k-1)/2,
Delta_k=C_k+Gamma_k-JS_k+Delta_(k-1)/2.
Multiplying by w_k and telescoping proves Delta_n+J_n=G_n+C_n and H_n=B_n^tree+J_n with no unpaid leaf silently discarded. Directly telescoping the phi formula yields the closed G_n above.

For h(t)/t=log2(1/t)+L+e(t), elementary logarithm inequalities give -Lt<=e(t)<=0, including t=1 by continuity. Since sum_k d_k<2, the weighted marginal-entropy sum is
S_n/d=n(n-1)/2+nL+E_n, -2L<=E_n<=0.
This proves the upper G_n bound. The lower bound follows from phi'(t)=1+log2[(1-t)/(1-2t)]>=1, hence phi(t)>=t. No numerical extrapolation establishes either bound.

For k>=3, beta_k<=3/8. Integrating the same derivative and using ln(1+u)<=u proves 0<=phi(x)-x<=2Lx^2 on [0,3/8]. Therefore
2-2d_k<=JS_k/d_k<=2+(8L-2)d_k.
The exact k=2 endpoint has JS_2/d_2=2. Summation gives the displayed J_n bounds. The existing exact H_k formula, with its empty contribution enclosed by elementary logs, gives
H_n/d=4(n-1)+2L+E_H,n,
-[2(n-1)+4L]d<=E_H,n<=0.
This is used for release/error quantification, not presented as a new class entropy theorem. It yields
Delta_n/I_n=n-5+L+o(1),
G_n/I_n=(n-1)/2+O(1/n),
C_n/I_n=(n-1)/2+L-2+O(1/n),
J_n/I_n=2+O(1/n),
B_n^tree/I_n=2+O(1/n).
The explicit bounds in DERIVATION.md include an all-n enclosure for C_n, so every asymptotic has a proved uniform error.

At k>=2, apply the actual coherent kernel to the parent residual product reference. The reference outcome probability of the constant child is
q_k=(1-d_k)^(k-1)/(2q0_k),
giving O_k=-log2[4q_k(1-q_k)]/2.
A039's signed Bayes-reference correction is independently reconstructed on actual child atoms:
rho_k=O_k+JS_k-Gamma_k.
For k>=3, q_k lies in [1/4,1/2], epsilon_k=1-2q_k<=2(k-2)d_k, and
O_k<= (8L/3)(k-2)^2 d_k^2.
The k=1,2 output charges vanish. Since sum_{k>=3}(k-2)^2 d_k=3, summing gives O_n<=8Ld. Thus R_n=O_n+J_n-G_n and R_n/Delta_n -> -1/2. The same bounds prove R_n<0 for all n>=9.

Finite normalization matters: n=2 has Delta=0, G=J=1 and C=0. It would be false to call Gamma and mutual information two nonnegative shares of root Delta at every finite n. Only the retained-JS identity and its asymptotic proportions are proved.

The first unsupported universal line is paying NET cumulative erased-label/witness cost plus terminal core entropy on overlapping activated sources or arbitrary Boolean functions with one nonreused physical influence budget. Here S_n-C_n=B_n^tree has a linear influence ratio even while G_n and C_n each have diverging ratios. Bounding a positive reference component again is not that missing payment.

# A039: information projection under coherent physical restrictions

The assigned projection target is resolved at the compensated-identity scope. Neither direction of unrestricted projection-KL monotonicity holds. Both proposed local Gamma payments in B08's new notes are refuted by actual Boolean functions. Full uniform-cube FEI remains OPEN. The new theorem awaits independent review.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For every Boolean sign f on every finite uniform cube, and every actual coordinate restriction, retain signed child coefficients before squaring. Let P=fhat^2, d_j=Inf_j(f), TC(f)=D_2(P||product Bern(d_j)), B_i=H(S_i|S_-i), JS_i=H(P_-i)-average H(P_child), and Gamma_i=sum_{j!=i}[h2(d_j)-average h2(d_j^child)]. All quantities are in bits. Write I_h=I_P(S_i;S_-i), distinct from physical total influence. We prove

    TC(f)=I_h-JS_i+Gamma_i+average TC(f_child).

For a finite adaptive fair tree with physical-history-measurable choices, the exact sum is

    sum_h w_h I_h + sum_h w_h Gamma_i + E_leaf TC
      = TC(root)+sum_h w_h JS_i.

We also price the Bayes reference reset exactly, retain K_full=v K_occ+Delta for the nonempty occupancy projection, and refute every finite fixed coefficient in Gamma_i<=c Inf_i or Gamma_i<=c(JS_i+Inf_i). Forced coordinates and zero spectral fibers use the explicit supported conventions in DERIVATION.md. These results do not claim that an independently regenerated witness law equals the Bayes posterior of its parent.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

DERIVATION.md gives the complete source-independent argument. Parent erasure first subtracts h2(d_i)-B_i from product KL. Recombining the actual coherent child mixture adds JS_i to the average fixed-reference KL. Reoptimizing the child products saves exactly Gamma_i, the sum of marginal Bernoulli KLs. Combining these three equalities proves the ledger. No classical phase-blind physical channel is assumed.

Apply the same function-specific kernel k(y|T)=P_child_y(T)/(2P_-i(T)) to both the residual spectrum and its parent product reference. If Q_y denotes its Bayes-predicted child reference, R_y the newly projected product, D_out=D_2(fair||Q_Y), and delta_y=E_child log2(Q_y/R_y), the reset balance is

    average delta_y = D_out+JS_i-Gamma_i.

Thus channel injection is zero for this explicit transport, and reference reoptimization is signed and priced. The zero-fiber kernel extension affects D_out and delta individually but not their difference. A038's occupancy projection splits TC into v K_occ+Delta, with Delta=D_2((1-v)delta_empty+v Q_r||product(d))>=0; its average physical change is the exact signed expression (4) in DERIVATION.md. Root rarity, conditional tilt and terminal costs are all retained.

Isospectral splits have JS_i=Gamma_i=0 and TC(parent)-TC(child)=I_h>=0. Conversely zero JS_i forces equal child spectra. Product TC, occupancy KL and Delta can each increase or decrease in expectation in the general case: K4's four-bit quadratic has zero parent values but positive child values; its triangle quadratic child has positive occupancy KL and Delta but uniform two-bit bent children with zero values. A three-bit point defect independently gives a strictly positive-to-zero TC decrease certified by 3^9<2^16.

For the m-bit point defect, a=2^(1-m), all coordinate influences equal a, Gamma_i/a=(m-1)+O(m a), and JS_i<=H_m-(1/2)H_(m-1)=4a+O(m a^2). The explicit errors in DERIVATION.md prove Gamma_i/(JS_i+Inf_i)->infinity and Gamma_i/Inf_i->infinity. This refutes both late lead helpers even if one may choose any coordinate. Also K_occ=0 throughout this control-first chain and Delta=TC, so

    [Delta_m-(1/2)Delta_(m-1)]/a
       =2m-6+log2(e)+R_m, |R_m|<=(12m+16)a.

This rules out an absolute O(removed influence) price for the occupancy normalization transfer. It does not refute FEI: its actual entropy decrement remains O(Inf_i).

For sparse activated bent surgery, DERIVATION.md states exact rational spectral classes, means and influence vectors for the selector core and both bent children. Control-first weighted sums telescope to the retained terminal core account; selector-first terms use the actual coherent child spectra. The finite rational intervals compute Delta and Bayes reset terms at k=4,6,8. At k=8,r=16, the control-first selector-core Delta release divided by removed influence lies in [32.8157957112,32.8157957113], while its Bayes reset divided by removed influence lies in [-17.2833779203,-17.2833779202]. The selector query Delta release lies in [15.1620621195,15.1620621196], and its Bayes reset lies in [2.7894444163,2.7894444164]. Signed cancellation is essential; neither component is a fresh influence allowance.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

For f_m=1-2*1_0, all queries are symmetric and the only nonconstant child is f_(m-1). With a=2^(1-m), B_m=phi((1-a)^2,a^2)+(2^(m-1)-1)*2a^2 and V_m=B_m+V_(m-1)/2, V_1=0.
